{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011CeNPK2owU6iwZUgSfe9Sm","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01BiLNzEHwiS7QTafMd7L29v","name":"Bash","input":{"command":"cd /var/folders/s4/by90nw7j7_l_nvvqs3hd9mjh0000gn/T/nurb-eval-trial-k6q1yd5j/project && cat > parts/pole_rest.md <<'MD'\n# pole_rest\n\n## What it is\n\nA bench rest that cradles a freshly finished pole while it dries. Several identical\nrests stand in a row and the pole lies across them, so the seat is an interface: the\npole's axis sits exactly 18.0mm above the bench, centred over the footprint, running\nalong Y. The seat is a half-round trough cut to the pole's own radius plus 0.25mm, so\nthe pole is carried on a wrapped arc rather than balanced on two edges, and the soft\nfinish never bears on a corner.\n\n## Design notes\n\n- **The seat is the pole's circle plus 0.25mm.** That gap is bigger than the 0.1mm the\n  finish needs to stay untouched and smaller than the 0.4mm at which a cradle stops\n  cradling and becomes a pair of edges. It holds a 168 degree contact arc down the whole\n  22mm of the rest.\n- **The seat's widest point lands exactly at the axis, z = 18.0.** The mouth is therefore\n  the widest part of the trough and the pole lowers straight down into it with nothing\n  to catch on. Nothing on the part rises above the axis, so the pole is half exposed to\n  the air, which is the point of a drying rest.\n- **`POLE_AXIS_HEIGHT` is a module constant, not a parameter.** Every rest in the row has\n  to hand the pole to the next one at the same height; it is the one dimension a user\n  must not be able to drag. Everything else is derived from `pole_diameter`, so a\n  different pole re-cuts the seat without moving the axis.\n- **3mm of wall beside the seat.** The support the pole needs is 1.2mm of material behind\n  the contact; 3mm carries that everywhere on the arc, survives the two 1mm chamfers at\n  the rim, and still leaves a 1mm flat on top rather than a knife edge.\n- **Solid below the seat.** 7.75mm of floor under the trough at the 20mm pole. Coring it\n  out would save about 500mm3 and buy a bridged roof to print around; the part is\n  6816mm3 as it stands, which is already light for its footprint.\n- **`reject` guards the fixed axis.** Past about a 31mm pole the seat would cut through\n  the bench-side floor at an 18mm axis height, and a rest with no floor is not a smaller\n  rest, it is a different design.\n\n## Don't\n\n- **Don't chamfer the seat's two end circles.** Polish takes every other exposed edge,\n  but a chamfer on a circle raises a cone that collides with the rim chamfer running\n  beside it and leaves four 0.9mm2 slivers, one at each top corner. Excluding the two\n  circular edges (`GeomType.LINE` filter) is what makes `nurb check` clean, and it also\n  keeps the full-radius seat running the entire length of the rest.\n- **Don't chamfer the outer bottom edges.** They lie in the bed face; a sloped chamfer\n  arriving at the plate lays a knife edge into the first layer. The vertical corner\n  chamfers are deliberate and stay.\n- **Don't turn the axis height into a parameter** to make a taller rest. A taller rest is\n  a different row of rests; this one mates with the ones already on the bench.\n- **Don't tighten `seat_clearance` toward 0.1** for a snugger hold. The pole is lowered in\n  and lifted out with a wet finish on it; 0.25mm is the gap that never wipes the finish\n  and still reads as a cradle to the support check.\n- **Don't core the floor with a tunnel along Y.** Tried on paper: a 10mm wide 45 degree\n  tunnel saves 550mm3 and leaves 2.75mm between its apex and the seat, which is inside\n  the wall rule but spends the part's whole floor margin for 8% of its volume.\n\n## Changelog\n\n- 2026-08-24: First version. 20mm pole, 22mm long, seat at 18.0mm axis height,\n  6816mm3, `nurb check` clean.\n\n```toml\n[part]\n\n[variants.pole_rest_dowel_16]\nnote = \"The same rest cut for a 16mm dowel: narrower seat, same 18mm axis height so it stands in the same row.\"\n\n[variants.pole_rest_dowel_16.params]\npole_diameter = 16.0\n\n[variants.pole_rest_25]\nnote = \"The same rest cut for a 25mm pole, which is about as fat as this seat height takes.\"\n\n[variants.pole_rest_25.params]\npole_diameter = 25.0\n```\nMD\nnurb check && echo \"=== card ===\" && nurb card pole_rest && nurb verify pole_rest","description":"Write card with variants, check, verify"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1147,"cache_read_input_tokens":67301,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":1147},"output_tokens":3,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"ecc56014-285b-415c-b37e-195e983ef10a","uuid":"147a75dc-a6c6-47a5-951c-a40da176e40a","timestamp":"2026-08-24T22:47:07.569Z","request_id":"req_011CeNPK1d18WR31MJ5PBNmH"}

format\n--- final part ---\nfrom nurb import *\n\n# Every rest in the row hands the pole to the next one, so the seat height is an\n# interface, not a preference: the axis sits exactly this far above the bench.\nPOLE_AXIS_HEIGHT = 18.0\n\n\n@part\ndef pole_rest(\n    pole_diameter=measured(\"pole_diameter\"),\n    rest_length=22.0,\n    seat_clearance=0.25,\n    wall_thickness=3.0,\n    chamfer_size=1.0,\n    draft=False,\n):\n    \"\"\"A cradle that holds a freshly finished pole off the bench while it dries.\n\n    pole_diameter: how thick the pole is across, measured with calipers\n    rest_length: how far the rest runs along the pole\n    seat_clearance: the gap between the seat and the finish, so nothing rubs\n    wall_thickness: how much material stands beside the seat at the rim\n    chamfer_size: how big the bevel on the handled edges is\n    \"\"\"\n    pole_radius = pole_diameter / 2.0\n    seat_radius = pole_radius + seat_clearance\n    floor = POLE_AXIS_HEIGHT - seat_radius\n    if floor < 2.5:\n        reject(\n            \"a pole this thick would have its seat cut through the bench-side floor \"\n            f\"at the fixed dries.\n\n    pole_diameter: how thick the pole is across, measured with calipers\n    rest_length: how far the rest runs along the pole\n    seat_clearance: the gap between the seat and the finish,
